Challenge: PanelAudit (formerly HistoSort)
4/26/2026

for now:
- please clean up the entire dataset description formatting, not just the table. everything looks messy and hard to read right now, it feels like raw copy paste without checking layout
- remove unnecessary or bloated sections. there's too much information that doesn't help understanding and just makes it harder to follow
- the table specifically is broken and needs to be properly formatted
- "tile perturbation (applied at prepare-time, cannot be undone)" doesn't need to be mentioned. it leaks how the data was prepared and blurs the line between valid vs invalid approaches
- move the "what not to use" section into the main description for now. don't rely on it separately since it isn't actually enforced in the agent instructions right now

Lessons for future challenges (do not repeat):
1. Tables on the platform's renderer break when any cell is too wide or contains long URLs / markdown links. Rule: keep every table cell under ~30 characters, never put `[label](url)` syntax inside a cell, put long descriptions as plain prose under the table, paste raw URLs in fenced code blocks under the table — not inside cells.
2. Do not paste markdown content blindly. After writing, check that line lengths are reasonable and that no table row has a single cell that dwarfs the others. The reviewer can tell when you have not eyeballed the layout.
3. Cut redundant sections aggressively. If two tables convey the same information one way and prose-form another way, keep one. The reviewer reads "raw copy paste without checking" as low effort.
4. Never describe data-preparation specifics in the participant-facing challenge description. Phrases like "applied at prepare-time", "cannot be undone", "downscale to 32×32", "JPEG quality 30", "HSV jitter Δ ≤ 0.45", "Gaussian σ ≈ 15/255", "irreversible perturbation" all leak how the data was made and blur valid-vs-invalid approaches. The challenge prompt should describe what the solver sees, not what the author did. Generic statements like "tiles have been processed so pixel-level matching against external collections is not feasible" are fine.
5. The "What Not To Use" section must be inline inside `### Overview` of the challenge description as a bullet list — not a separate trailing section. Reviewers said the platform does not enforce a separate section in agent instructions, so anything you want enforced must be in the main description prose.
6. The dataset form fill (DATASET_FORM_FILL.md) is internal until the challenge closes, so it can mention the upstream source by name (e.g. "PatchCamelyon", "H&E"). The challenge description (CHALLENGE_FORM_FILL.md) is participant-facing and must stay source-neutral.

brianaltan
Revision Requested

Challenge: Synthetic Morphological Inflection Engine (Synthetic Contextual Sequence Transduction)
4/26/2026

i dont really like the setup for this challenge. after you fix everything i'll need to rerun 3 agents. synthetic data can give low-variance scoring if not handled properly
- why are you leaking how the test set is constructed? remove all of this. describing held-out roots, contexts, feature cells, and exact proportions exposes the evaluation design and shouldn't be visible
- same issue with the transformation rules. don't list or hint at the internal rule system (harmony, assimilation, etc.). that's effectively revealing the solution structure
- remove the training noise details as well. specifying exact noise types and percentages leaks how the data was generated and how to exploit it
- fix the table display using a format supported by the platform. if that's not possible, remove the table — the current version looks broken and hurts readability
- add a "what to use" section directly inside the main challenge description (intended vs not intended approaches), not under a separate category. right now that guidance isn't embedded, so agents won't see it properly

Lessons for future synthetic challenges (do not repeat):
1. For SYNTHETIC challenges, do not describe how the test set was constructed in the participant-facing description. No held-out roots / held-out contexts / held-out feature cells / split proportions / coverage percentages / "X out of Y combinations" wording. Anything that lets the solver reason about which inputs are systematically held back is a leak of the evaluation design.
2. Do not list or hint at the internal rule system that generates the labels. For a hidden-rule synthetic challenge, every named rule (e.g. "vowel harmony", "boundary assimilation", "hiatus resolution", "epenthetic insertion", "sandhi", "allomorphy", "portmanteau", "suppletion", "ablaut") is a hint at the solution structure. Keep the description abstract: "a hidden multi-step transformation conditioned on the input tokens", and let the solver discover the rules.
3. Do not state the exact noise type and percentage on training labels. Phrases like "approximately 10% of training labels contain single-character noise" leak both how the data was generated and how to exploit it (e.g. set up an aggressive denoiser tuned to single-character substitutions). It is fine to mention the existence of label noise as an irreducible error floor, without specifying the mechanism or rate.
4. Total raw counts (training rows, test rows, total raw rows) are typically OK to mention because they are observable from the CSVs anyway, but anything beyond that — held-out structure, generative rule names, noise mechanism / rate — is treated as a leak.
5. Embed the "what to use / what not to use" guidance INSIDE the main challenge description as a bullet list under the Overview, in addition to (or in place of) any separate "What Not To Use" form field. The platform does not always pass the separate field through to agent instructions, so anything that must be enforced has to be in the prose participants and agents actually see.
6. Drop the entire `## Rubrics` section. The current platform schema (per LATEST.md) does not use rubrics; including them clutters the description and confuses validators.
7. Drop the `## Grading` YAML block from the description. Grade direction / min / max go in the form's Grading Configuration field, not in the prose description.
8. The challenge `prepare(...)` function MUST use the standard signature `prepare(raw: Path, public: Path, private: Path) -> None` — never a custom one with default folder names like `prepare(raw_dir="raw_data", public_dir="pub", private_dir="priv")`. The platform calls `prepare(raw, public, private)` directly and a non-standard signature will silently mis-route outputs.

6/11/2026

Review by

aikyatan
Revision Requested
1. Grader Script Fixes
Fix the Kendall's tau_b Tie-Handling Calculation: The custom _kendall_tau function skips updating tie counts (tie_x and tie_y) when a pair matches exactly in both variables, which mathematically deviates from standard implementations like scipy.stats.kendalltau.
Simplify the condition block to track variable ties independently before assessing concordance or discordance.
2. Prepare Script Fixes
Fix the NumPy TypeError : This is performing a bitwise XOR (^) between a signed Python int (SPLIT_SEED) and a NumPy uint64 object, which triggers an immediate script crash in modern NumPy environments.
Replace np.uint64(0x1111_1111) with a native Python integer literal (0x1111_1111).
Mismatched Dataset Validation Bounds :The script passes datasets with >= 100 volumes initially, but immediately crashes if the total volume count is under 109 because the required 18% test split falls below the hard coded limit of 20 minimum test samples (n_test < 20).
Increase the initial dataset volume check to require len(volumes) < 110, or decrease the split cutoff constraint down to 15.
Documentation Improvements
Disclose Hidden Left-Right Mirror Inversions: The _perturb_slice engine randomly mirrors slices on the horizontal axis with a 50% probability. This drastically disrupts 3D structural continuity across the volume, yet it is completely unmentioned in the challenge description.
Explicitly document independent left-right slice flipping under the data augmentation overview section so participants know to design models around it.

Revision Requested
The problem description explicitly states that a scene can have 0 fins (e.g., the example 6002,0,0.0,45.0,"[]"). However, the prepare.py script makes the fatal assumption that every scene exists in objects.csv.
Use .get() with an empty DataFrame fallback to gracefully handle scenes without objects

Ensure the mapping is completely detached from any original sequence by explicitly shuffling the scenes before assigning IDs.

In the main evaluation loop, the grader successfully aligns the submission DataFrame to match the ground truth rows perfectly
But immediately afterward, it uses an O(N) lookup operation inside an O(N) loop to find the row it already aligned:
for _, arow in ans.iterrows():
    srow = sub.loc[sub["id"] == arow["id"]].iloc[0]

you can iterate through them concurrently.
for (_, arow), (_, srow) in zip(ans.iterrows(), sub.iterrows()):

aikyatan
Revision Requested
In prepare.py, you enforce a standard image size by resizing any image that is not exactly 256x256 pixels
You must capture the original (width, height) before resizing and multiply cx and cy by IMG_SIZE / original_width and IMG_SIZE / original_height, respectively.
Your _find_image function uses a wildcard glob and blindly grabs the first sorted result
Restrict your glob search specifically to valid image extensions (e.g., raw.glob(f"images/{scene_hash}.[pj]*")).

In _macro_f1, you explicitly force "NONE" to be an evaluated class to prevent competitors from coasting
Standard F1 implementations treat True Negatives on an absent class as a perfect score (or dynamically exclude the class from the denominator) if TP + FP + FN == 0.

In _brier_skill, you safeguard against zero-variance in the test set labels
Instead of relying on the empirical test-set mean for your reference model, lock ref to a static, theoretical climatology base-rate (e.g., $0.5$), ensuring mse_ref never hits a zero-variance floor.

In prepare.py, you assign sequential id integers to train and test sets by iterating over lists derived from scenes.sort_values("scene_hash")
Shuffle the scenes dataframe using a seeded random state before creating your id_map

Lessons for future synthetic challenges (do not repeat):
1. Dataset SIZE matters to reviewers independently of correctness: a few hundred scenes reads as "too small" even when the task is sound. For self-rendered synthetic CV challenges, target ~1.5-3k scenes (still tiny on disk: thousands of small JPEGs are well under the size budget). Do not ship a default generate.py that yields only ~650 scenes.
2. A medium-CV agent score in the low-mid 0.8s reads as "not much room left to improve / possibly rule-based." Build a real human>agent gap: increase occlusion so supporting/buried structure must be INFERRED (not read off), add more objects/denser piles, and lean on the irreducible-ambiguity fraction so even a strong solver is capped well below 1.0. Re-check the realistic-ceiling estimate after toughening.
3. "Make scenes more realistic" is a recurring reviewer/author ask for abstract synthetic renders. Add a container/bin, contact shadows, varied object sizes, and depth cues so the scene reads as a real settled pile rather than floating primitives - without breaking the exact ground-truth (keep geometry analytic).

1. Provide a _analyze.py "strong-solver" estimate as evidence the ceiling is < 0.7 with headroom.


sprint_3\Inverse Shadow Casting And Illumination Recovery (platform pre-submission checks, self-discovered):
Two automated checks interact and can be mutually contradictory if you try to game one of them:
  * "Grade Sample Validation" - runs grade.py on the provided sample_submission.csv and FAILS if the sample scores at (or effectively at) the declared theoretical MINIMUM. The sample must be a valid, NON-DEGENERATE baseline that scores strictly and comfortably above the minimum.
  * "Grade Script Score Bounds" - independently verifies the declared theoretical minimum/maximum match what grade.py can ACTUALLY return. The true minimum is 0.0 (the grader returns 0.0 on empty / invalid / id-mismatched submissions) and the true maximum is 1.0 (a perfect submission). It FAILS if you declare a minimum other than 0 (e.g. "Expected minimum score to be 0, but got 0.0909...").

Lessons for future synthetic challenges (do not repeat):
1. DO NOT fix a failing "Grade Sample Validation" by raising the declared theoretical minimum to match a weak sample score. It will pass the sample check but then FAIL "Grade Script Score Bounds", because the grader's real minimum is 0.0. Always declare min=0.0, max=1.0 to match what grade.py can output.
2. The ONLY correct fix is to make the sample_submission.csv itself score comfortably above 0 (accepted challenges' samples land ~0.12-0.17; aim for >=~0.12, not ~0.09). Do this WITHOUT reading images: fill the sample with sensible LABEL PRIORS (median count, median/mean of continuous targets, a spread layout) - never an all-empty / all-zero submission.
3. Watch the metric's partial-credit shaping. Squaring every sub-score (SCORE_POWER=2.0) crushes an honest mean/median-prior baseline below the platform's effective ~0.1 floor. Use a milder power (e.g. 1.6): it still rewards near-exact answers and keeps the strong-solver ceiling < 0.7, but lifts the legitimate weak baseline into the accepted range. Verify with a sweep that (oracle=1.0, strong-solver<0.7, sample>=~0.12) all hold simultaneously.
4. To find the right sample baseline target, prepare an ALREADY-ACCEPTED challenge locally and grade its sample_submission.csv - that number is the empirical "passing" floor to match.

sprint_3\3d_challenges\Retrodiction Drop Order From A Settled Pile (title now: Box-Pile Drop Order and Structural Collapse Reconstruction):

6/16/2026

Resolution (by own decision - "make it novel by your own decisions"):
Added a genuinely NEW capability axis instead of rewording: a per-object STRUCTURAL COUNTERFACTUAL ("remove-one collapse") head. For each box, predict the set of boxes that lose ALL support to the floor (and fall) if it alone is removed. This requires recovering the full MULTI-SUPPORTER contact graph (a box on two independent supports survives losing one), which is strictly more 3D structure than the transitive drop-order pairs reveal - so the two heads probe different capabilities from the same two occluded views. Reweighted correctness = 0.40*S_po^2 + 0.30*S_coll^2 + 0.15*S_first^2 + 0.15*S_last^2 (S_coll = mean per-object Jaccard). Added a cite-and-distinguish "Relation to prior work" subsection (ShapeStacks/block-tower stability + temporal-order-from-static) explaining the three simultaneous differences (abstaining partial-order retrodiction, per-object counterfactual collapse, occlusion-limited 2-view input). Verified: oracle=1.0, sample-template=0.19, geometry-but-structure-naive shortcut=0.55 (<0.7 ceiling intact).

Lessons for future synthetic challenges (do not repeat):
1. To lift a LOW NOVELTY score the durable fix is to add a MEASURABLE NEW capability axis derived from data the simulation already computes (here: the support graph -> remove-one collapse), NOT to reword the description. Pick an axis that is ORTHOGONAL to the existing heads (cannot be derived from them) so the research check sees a genuinely new task, then reweight the metric and add a cite-and-distinguish "Relation to prior work" paragraph naming the nearest neighbour and the concrete differences.
2. You can add a new label to an already-RENDERED corpus WITHOUT re-rendering: write a small replay script (_regen_scenes.py) that re-runs the deterministic _sample_scene(seed, idx) for every scene, recomputes the new label from existing geometry, and ASSERTS every pre-existing column is byte-for-byte identical before overwriting scenes.csv. Keep the new computation rng-free (or after all rng draws) so determinism is preserved. Then re-run prepare.py and re-zip raw.
3. REPEAT-MISTAKE CAUGHT IN AUDIT (same as Folded Box-Net lesson #1): prepare.py wrote view paths as "views/<id>_vK.jpg" while the files were actually copied to public/train/views/ and public/test/views/. The CSV path MUST carry the split prefix ("train/views/..." / "test/views/...") so it resolves relative to the public/ root where the CSV lives. Build the per-split prefix from the same variable used to create the directory, and during the audit verify every view0/view1 path actually exists under public/.
4. When adding a JSON-object-valued head (id -> list-of-ids), reuse the standard guards from day one: cap raw string length (MAX_JSON_LEN), cap number of keys (MAX_COLLAPSE_KEYS), cap per-token length (MAX_TOKEN_LEN), and score empty-vs-empty as 1.0 so "supports nothing" is only credited when predicted empty (prevents a blanket "everything above falls" exploit).

6/19/2026

Resolution (novelty upgrade):
Added counterfactual intervention (intervene_fork input + query_bin_if_flip) and inverse retrodiction (init_states_json). Updated generate/grade/prepare/docs, rebuilt fliprun_raw.zip. Verified: perfect=1.0, sample<0.5, prepare video-existence filter.

Platform check (Agent runs & difficulty), 6/20/2026:
Agent runs scored 0.9041 / 0.9041 / 0.9104 -> "Agent run 1,2,3 reached the configured difficulty threshold." (too easy). Asked to bring agent best <= 0.6.
ROOT CAUSE (important, generalises): in a PERFECT BINARY TREE the landing-bin index IS the root-to-leaf path written in binary (j = 2j + d). So watching each marble land fully reveals the path / lean of every fork it visited, and the flip-flop dynamics are deterministic -> the whole task is READ-OFF + bookkeeping, not inference. The ONLY genuine difficulty is forks no shown marble ever visited (their lean is unknowable). On the old params that unvisited fraction was tiny, so a near-perfect solver (and the agents) reached ~0.95.
Also found a SPEC BUG: switch_states_json label was the state after ALL n_total marbles (through the query), but the prompt asked for "AFTER the shown marbles". Fixed the label to states_after_shown so prompt and label agree.
FIX (raise irreducible ambiguity, don't fake it):
- generate.py: depth 3/4 -> 4/5 (weighted 0.22/0.78; 15 or 31 forks, 16/32 bins); n_balls_shown 4-6 (fewer); query_offset 6-10 (long horizon). Few marbles + deep tree + long roll-out => the forward query and counterfactual routinely pass through unvisited forks => genuinely uncertain. layout_family coarsened to depth{4,5} (healthy worst-group buckets). n_base default 450->700 (1400 clips, addresses "too small").
- grade.py: reweight onto the ambiguity-capped heads: switch 0.20->0.12, init 0.15->0.13, bins 0.15->0.05 (bins is a pure re-read of visible landings, must not prop the score), query 0.25->0.35, qflip 0.25->0.35; calib 0.10->0.08.
- MEASUREMENT METHOD (reusable): wrote _analyze.py = an OPTIMAL-SOLVER oracle. It perfectly reads every shown landing bin (so it reconstructs every VISITED fork exactly) and simulates the deterministic dynamics, but must guess unvisited forks uniformly; Monte-Carlo over the guesses gives the EXPECTED best-possible score. Init states are sampled uniformly so uniform guessing is provably optimal -> this is a true UPPER BOUND on any agent. Tuned params+weights via _sweep.py until that ceiling ~0.57. Final on the real prepared test set: perfect(labels)=1.0, OPTIMAL ceiling=0.569, sample baseline=0.188. Agents (perception error on top of the ceiling) therefore land <=0.6.
PROACTIVE prev-mistake fixes applied here too (same as Coupled Oscillator / Shape Under Cloth):
- ID split-boundary leak: prepare.py assigned train ids 0..M-1 then test M..M+T-1 (id leaked the split). Replaced with ONE globally-shuffled id pool (GLOBAL_ID_SEED) over train+test so id<->clip is split-agnostic (verified train/test id ranges now interleave). Sorted all output CSVs by id.
- MIN_GROUP_TEST=20 guard (enforced only when test>=100 so the tiny smoke run still passes): every worst-group bucket (split_group/ood_axis/arena_style) must clear the floor; smallest real bucket = 53.
- Checkpoints: data leakage (fixed id leak; scaffold split by base_scenario_id keeps both variants same side; uniform init = no learnable prior), baseline not too high (0.188), class distribution OK (depth 106/394 test, uniform L/R labels, buckets>=53), no missing data (0 NaN in train/test/answers), What-not-to-use present.
- NOTE on video split-prefix lesson: did NOT add a train/ test/ prefix here. Agents had already scored 0.91 (videos loaded), proving THIS challenge resolves video paths relative to the split subdir (video col = "videos/<id>.mp4"); adding the prefix would BREAK it. The split-prefix rule is platform/layout-specific, not universal.
- Dataset form cleanup: removed scoring/grader references from DATASET_FORM_FILL Notes (rule: dataset form is a pure data dictionary, no scoring/grade/prepare/title mentions).

FOLLOW-UP (platform Prepare failed 6/19/2026): the reviewer's exact streaming snippet — iio.imopen(dst,"w",plugin="pyav") + init_video_stream("libx264",...) + iio.imiter(src,plugin="pyav") — CRASHED on the grader image at imopen.py (the pyav WRITE plugin is not installed there). Fix: keep the streaming requirement but drive the FFMPEG backend (which is the same one that DECODES the clips, so it is always present) via imageio v2 streaming reader/writer:
    import imageio.v2 as iio2
    reader = iio2.get_reader(src); fps = float(reader.get_meta_data().get("fps",10) or 10)
    writer = iio2.get_writer(dst, fps=fps, codec="libx264", quality=7, macro_block_size=16)
    for frame in reader: writer.append_data(frame)   # lazy ffmpeg pipe -> O(1) frames in memory
    writer.close(); reader.close()
Verified locally on a generated 20-frame clip (20 in -> 20 out, fps preserved). Synced PASTE_THIS_PREPARE.txt.
Lesson (do not repeat): do NOT use plugin="pyav" for VIDEO WRITING on the grader image — pyav's write path is not available and imopen raises. Stream through the imageio ffmpeg backend (v2 get_reader/get_writer append_data loop) instead: it both streams frame-by-frame AND uses the backend already present for decoding. Reviewers accept "stream the frames iteratively" — they did not mandate pyav specifically.

Lesson (do not repeat): for any BSS / skill-score metric whose reference is a "climatology", the reference MUST be precomputed from TRAIN and embedded as a constant in grade.py — never recomputed from the graded answers (that leaks test statistics and makes the baseline shift per grading set). BUT switching to a static train reference can drop a marginal sample_submission to exactly 0.0 (the declared min) and fail Grade Sample Validation; ship a slightly richer conditional+shrunk marginal (metadata-only, no labels/peaks) so the sample lands just above 0. Always cap free-text numeric cells by length BEFORE splitting/parsing.

sprint_3\3d_challenges\claude_charitha\Coupled Oscillator Hidden Coupling Graph Inference:
Review by brianaltan — Revision Requested (6/19/2026)  [SECOND review, after aikyatan's]
1. Prepare the dataset (re-run prepare).
2. grade.py: a malformed strongest_pair parses to None, and on no-edge rows that can score as CORRECT — invalid JSON is too forgiving for that head.
3. grade.py: phases accept any numeric value, not just [0,360). Modulo scoring makes it fine, but the prompt/grader should be consistent.

6/19/2026

Resolution (all implemented + verified):
1. strongest_pair head (_parse_pair_single + _strong_score): added an EMPTY_PAIR sentinel that is returned ONLY for a well-formed empty JSON list []. Malformed / unparseable / wrong-shape cells still return None. _strong_score now: when gold has no pair (EMPTY_PAIR), it scores 1.0 ONLY if pred == EMPTY_PAIR (explicit []); a malformed None pred now scores 0.0 (previously the old `gold is None -> 1.0 if pred is None` branch handed malformed cells a free pass on no-edge rows). When gold is a real pair, both None and EMPTY_PAIR pred score 0.0. NOTE: current corpus has 0 zero-edge scenes (min n_edges=1), so this bug never bit the live answers, but the prompt explicitly allows [] so the fix is required for correctness. Verified: no-edge gold + malformed pred = 0.0 (was 1.0); no-edge gold + [] = 1.0; real gold + [1,2]=1.0, +[1,3]=0.5, +malformed=0.0, +[]=0.0.
2. phase head (_phase_score): predicted phase is now explicitly wrapped pv = pv % 360.0 before _circ_dist (behaviour was already modulo-equivalent; this makes the grader's documented convention explicit). Updated the PROMPT (prepare.py) and the description (field list, S_phase prose, submission notes) to state values outside [0,360) are wrapped modulo 360, so prompt and grader now agree. Verified: 370 vs 10 -> 1.0; -5 vs 355 -> 1.0.
3. Re-ran prepare clean (498 train / 252 test). perfect=1.0, sample=0.1309 (both unchanged by the fixes; sample's strongest "[]" still scores 0 because every answer has a real pair).
- Synced PASTE_THIS_GRADE.txt (full rewrite) + PASTE_THIS_PREPARE.txt (prompt). Updated CHALLENGE_FORM_FILL.md embedded grade.py (_parse_pair_single, _strong_score, _phase_score), embedded PROMPT, S_strong/S_phase formula prose, submission-notes bullets, graceful-degradation paragraph, and field description.

Lesson (do not repeat): when a parser maps BOTH a legitimate empty declaration ([]) AND malformed input to the same None, a downstream "None == correct on the empty case" branch silently rewards garbage. Use a distinct sentinel for the well-formed empty so malformed input can be scored 0. For any tolerant/modulo metric, make the normalization EXPLICIT in the grader (pv % 360) AND state it in the prompt/description so the documented contract matches the code.

[2026-07-05] Challenge: Audiobook Reader Verification and Unknown-Reader Detection
Reviewer: User correction on grading shape
Issue:
- The prior `RawFinal^64` strict curve was judged too artificial even though perfect still scored exactly 1.0.
- User requested a more direct approach that punishes each wrong answer more heavily, with the power-curve fallback reserved only if the direct approach fails in a later agent rerun.
Fix:
- Replaced the global power curve with a row-level exactness/error-budget metric: `StrictRow = max(0, 1 - 14*(1 - ExactRate))`, where a row is exact only if the provenance is exactly right and the known/unknown score is on the correct side of 0.5.
- Added `WorstStrictGroup` using the same penalty inside hidden robustness groups.
- New final formula: `0.45*StrictRow + 0.20*WorstStrictGroup + 0.10*ProvenanceF1 + 0.10*Knownness + 0.05*ReaderConsistency + 0.10*Calibration`.
- Verified sample 0.129113, perfect 1.0, cheap baselines weak, and paste files synced.

- Prefer direct row-level exactness and worst-group penalties before using global nonlinear score curves. If platform agents still exceed the threshold, then revisit data difficulty or a clearly documented stricter transformation.

- The observed rerun scores would map to about 0.1269, 0.1250, and 0.2831 under the new curve.
Preventive Check:
- If a strict curve is needed after a direct penalty fails, avoid a nonzero global floor unless the declared theoretical minimum is changed. Prefer an anchored curve that keeps actual min=0.0, sample>=0.12, perfect=1.0, and documented high-agent proxies below the configured threshold.

[2026-08-03] Challenge: EEG-Guided Spontaneous Attention Path Reconstruction
Reviewer: Platform Trivial Issues
Issue:
- The ConsistencyScore definition introduced `P`, `T`, `C`, and edge-switch count `E` but did not explicitly say whether `E` was already included in `P`. The formula `2*(C+E)/(P+T+E)` therefore looked like possible double-counting.
Fix:
- Clarified that `E` is a subset of `P`: every edge switch is counted once as a submitted prediction. Each accepted edge switch is also matched to one virtual boundary reference transition because the state outside the episode is unobservable. The extra denominator `E` is that virtual reference count, while numerator `2E` is matched-pair credit; the submitted switch itself is not counted twice. Synchronized the local and Shipd descriptions and verified the clarification after a same-URL reload.
Preventive Check:
- Whenever an F1-style metric adds virtual reference objects, define all set relationships explicitly. State whether auxiliary counts are subsets of predicted/reference totals and explain separately what each numerator and denominator occurrence represents.

[2026-09-19] Challenge: Repair the Damaged Injury-Code Roster (v1 saturation repair)
Reviewer: Shipd Benchmark Calibration / creator trajectory audit
Issue:
- Live v1 scores were `0.9177`, `0.9178`, and `0.9159`; Benchmark Calibration failed because all three agents saturated.
- All supplied trajectories independently found the same shortcut: only 32 native code profiles existed, while 83.8% of hidden rosters had exactly one displayed card outside that set and it was always the damaged card. One agent's first learned baseline already scored `0.910365853658536`.
- A TF-IDF/set-feature profile classifier plus invalid-card detection, one-field repair enumeration, and Hungarian assignment therefore solved the intended localization problem nearly completely.
Fix:
- Rejected Gaussian/sinusoidal noise, stochastic penalties, caps, powers, and decorative distractors. Kept the smooth additive grader and exact schema unchanged.
- Changed corruption generation so the damaged card is always another valid native profile differing in exactly one field and absent only from the current roster. Every displayed card is globally plausible and all five are distinct.
- Selected cue signatures using training-source statistics only: at least two native profiles per cue and at most 98% dominance. The deterministic build retains 59,169 training-source records, 17,710 later-period candidates, 495 cue signatures, 5,000 train rosters, and 1,500 hidden rosters.
- Sample=`0.157766666667`, packaged CPU baseline=`0.633616666667`, and five trajectory-inspired replays range `0.627300`–`0.644233333333`; perfect remains `1.0`.
Preventive Check:
- For corruption/repair tasks, test global support membership before publication. If the damaged item is usually the only candidate outside the native vocabulary, localization is leaked even when row positions are shuffled. Require plausible closed-set corruptions and replay the exact agent decoder. Difficulty changes must be deterministic and source-grounded; never add arbitrary noise or manipulate the grader to push scores down.
